## 2. Partly parsed objects of `Playable_Batmobile_SF`

The card's own 2026-11-02 counts (Vehicle 4, Weapons 10, Projectiles 8, ~36 materials) are
not reproducible under any single definition I tried; the three definitions side by side,
so the discrepancy is visible rather than explained away:

| definition | Vehicle | Weapons | Projectiles | materials |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| tail>0 (any unparsed byte) | 70 | 23 | 40 | 454 |
| tail>2 (more than the terminator) | 9 | 10 | 8 | 61 |
| stop != eof (stream ended some other way) | 9 | 10 | 8 | 61 |

This file accounts for **every** object with `parsed < size` in the four groups (tail > 0), which is the superset; the tail classification below shows why the bigger
numbers are not 'more missing data': almost every one of them is the 2-byte terminator.

### Vehicle - 70 objects: terminator 61, terminator_early 9

| export | class | object | parsed/size | tail | tail kind | evidence |
|---:|---|---|---|---:|---|---|

## 3. The `BmScript.RVehicleBatmobile` defaults in `Startup.upk`

`Startup.upk`: 174,890 exports; BmScript.RVehicleBatmobile 97, BmScript.Default__RVehicleBatmobile 37

### 3a. The 97 property objects - where the values are **not**

Three measurements, each an oracle that can fail:

1. **Payload size is a function of the property's class.** derived: the payload size is a function of the property's class only (one size for all scalar properties, one for all pointer/array properties), so the payload is a descriptor, not a value store

| class | objects | payload sizes |
|---|---:|---|
| `Function` | 56 | {61: 1, 146: 1, 943: 1, 410: 1, 73: 2, 290: 1, 162: 1, 138: 1, 98: 2, 69: 1, 394: 1, 465: 1, 176: 1, 219: 1, 247: 1, 118: 1, 115: 1, 164: 1, 163: 1, 156: 2, 345: 1, 406: 1, 64: 1, 101: 1, 336: 1, 779: 1, 966: 1, 54: 1, 495: 2, 1661: 1, 3336: 1, 197: 1, 39: 1, 45: 1, 193: 1, 278: 1, 191: 1, 1082: 1, 240: 1, 396: 1, 209: 1, 72: 1, 58: 1, 78: 1, 77: 1, 316: 1, 89: 1, 400: 1, 200: 1, 103: 1, 963: 1, 102: 1} |
| `ObjectProperty` | 25 | {34: 25} |
| `FloatProperty` | 6 | {30: 6} |
| `BoolProperty` | 5 | {30: 5} |
| `ArrayProperty` | 3 | {34: 3} |
| `IntProperty` | 1 | {30: 1} |
| `ComponentProperty` | 1 | {34: 1} |

   scalar properties: 12, size(s) [[30]]; pointer/array properties: 29, size(s) [[34]] -> **holds: True**

2. **Three same-class properties are byte-identical apart from the counter.** derived: the three payloads are byte-identical once the per-object counter at byte 2 is masked, so no field between them can be a per-property value

   names: `fAfterBurnerRumbleCutoffStartStrength`, `fAfterBurnerRumbleCutoffTimeMultiplier`, `fAfterBurnerRumbleStrengthTimeMultiplier` -> identical when byte 2..5 is masked: **True**

3. **The names are absent from the grab's tag streams**: 96 of the 97 names do NOT occur as a named property tag on any of the 20,128 objects (1 hit(s)).

   - `UpgradedGeneratorBonus` on `JokersBatmobile` (RVehicleBatmobile, Playable_BatmobileJoker_SF export 3180), ArrayProperty at class offset 8608

So the values are **not** in these objects: the payloads are descriptors whose layout is
fixed per property class, and three of them cannot hold a value at all. Conclusion:
**UNKNOWN where the defaults live**; they are not in the shipped packages' decodable
streams, and `BmScript` (where such defaults would be authored) is not on disk.

The decoded values are class offsets, not member names: `OFFSETS.json` has no layout for
`ParticleSystemComponent`, `CylinderComponent`, `SVectorWheel`, `StaticMeshComponent`, so
which field each offset is stays UNKNOWN (that is the BmSDK-AK layout question).

## 4. UNKNOWNs

1. **the values of the 97 `BmScript.RVehicleBatmobile` class-default properties** - their payloads are fixed-size descriptors (oracle 2: one size for all 12 scalar properties, one for all 29 pointer/array properties) and three same-class properties are byte-identical apart from a counter (oracle 3); 96 of the 97 names never appear as a named property tag anywhere in the 20,128-object grab (measured below). So the defaults are not in these objects and not in the grab's tag streams; they would be in the class's serialized instance data or in `BmScript` (a package that is not shipped)
2. **the value of `XrayLightComponent`'s `StructProperty` at class offset 0x200 and the `ObjectProperty` at 0x13c on every prefixed CDO component** - the tags decode (type, class offset, payload size) but `OFFSETS.json` has no member names for these component classes, so which field each offset is stays UNKNOWN - that is the BmSDK-AK layout question G3-01 raised
3. **objects that are not reachable from the decoded vehicle chain** - no decoded reference chain from `Playable_Batmobile_SF`'s own vehicle objects reaches them. That is not proof of irrelevance (a class default or a native lookup could use one); answering it needs the runtime oracle (G3-07)
4. **the 6 CDO component payloads past the 512 MiB decode budget** - `PartialPackage` refuses them by design rather than guessing; raising the budget would decompress ~645 MiB of the Startup image

## 5. Oracles and checks

`--check` re-derives both files and fails if they drift, and asserts:

- the untagged total is 925 and every one of them has a payload shape and a verdict;
- every partial object in the four groups has a tail classification;
- the CDO prefix oracle holds on every component payload of 46+ bytes;
- the class-size oracle holds (two sizes, split by scalar vs pointer properties);
- the three Rumble properties are identical once the counter is masked;
- the 97 property names are absent from the grab's named tags (the measurement above).
